Cats would never do that. My aunt had a cat that would literally hide under the couch and swipe at your ankles if you walked by. She called him Mr. Whiskers, which was such a cute name for such a mean cat. She ended up having to give him to a farm because he scratched the mailman. The mailman was so mad, he threatened to stop delivering their mail entirely...\n\nDoctor: Do you tend to avoid certain situations or tasks because you're worried about freezing up like you do at work?\n\nPatient: Avoid things? Um, no, I don't really think so. I mean, I still go to my shifts at the restaurant and do whatever I'm supposed to do. I mostly work the dinner rush anyway. We actually just changed our menu for the summer, which is always kind of a pain because we have to memorize all the new seasonal specials. \n\nThey added this peach and goat cheese salad thing. Peaches are really good right now, actually. I bought a whole basket of them at the farmer's market on Saturday. The guy selling them was wearing this huge straw hat, kind of like a sombrero, but made of woven grass or something. He said he grew them all in his own orchard down south. I tried to grow cherry tomatoes on my apartment balcony once, but the birds kept eating them before they even turned red. Pigeons are just so bold in the city, they'll walk right up to your feet. One time a pigeon actually walked right into the restaurant because the host left the front door propped open to get a breeze, and the manager was running around with a broom trying to shoo it back out. It knocked over a whole tray of water glasses... \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't avoid doing things or anything like that. I just do my normal routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind and won't go away?\n\nPatient: Repeating thoughts? Um, no, I don't really have anything like that. My mind is pretty quiet, mostly. I mean, sometimes I get songs stuck in my head, like everyone else. Yesterday I had that old commercial jingle for the carpet cleaning place stuck in my head all morning. \n\nYou know the one, with the little animated bubbles that sing the phone number? I haven't even seen that commercial in years, but it just popped in there while I was wiping down the tables before we opened. We have to use this new sanitizer spray now, it smells exactly like artificial green apple. It's so strong, it kind of gives me a headache if I don't open the window in the back station. The window looks out at the alley, and there's this one stray cat that always sits on top of the blue dumpster. It's an orange tabby, missing half of its left ear. I started bringing little pieces of leftover salmon from the kitchen to leave out for it. The chef got kind of annoyed about it at first, but then he started saving the scraps for me in a little plastic deli container. He's actually a really nice guy, he just yells a lot during service. He used to work at this fancy French place downtown before he came to us, and he still wears those checkered chef pants even though our uniform is just black jeans... \n\nBut no, I don't have any thoughts that won't go away. Just normal stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body, watching yourself do things?\n\nPatient: Outside my body? Um, no, I don't think I've ever felt like that. That sounds kind of like a movie or something. I mostly just feel like I'm right here in my own skin. \n\nSpeaking of skin, I actually had to switch my laundry detergent recently because my arms were getting all itchy. I used to use the one with the lavender scent, but I guess they changed the formula or something. It's so annoying when companies do that. You find something you like, and then they just go and mess with it. Like my favorite brand of coffee. They completely redesigned the bag, and for like two weeks I couldn't find it on the shelf at the grocery store. I had to ask the stock boy, and he was so young, he looked like he was maybe sixteen. He had these bright blue braces on his teeth. I remember when my sister got braces, she cried for three days straight because her mouth hurt so much. She wouldn't eat anything except mashed potatoes and vanilla pudding. My mom got so tired of making mashed potatoes that she bought this huge industrial-sized box of the instant kind, the flakes? They taste so fake, like cardboard, but my sister didn't even care. She just wanted something she didn't have to chew. She still eats them sometimes when she's stressed out...\n\nBut no, I don't feel like I'm outside my body or watching myself. I'm just me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a daze or disconnected from what's happening around you?\n\nPatient: Disconnected? Like in a daze? Um, no, I don't really get like that. I'm usually pretty focused on what's going on around me, especially at work. You kind of have to be, you know? \n\nEspecially when it gets busy. Like last Friday, we had this massive party of twelve come in right at 7:00 without a reservation. They were celebrating a birthday, I think it was the grandfather's 80th or something. They brought in this huge sheet cake from that bakery down on 4th Street. The one with the pink awning? They make the best buttercream frosting. I actually went there for my roommate's birthday last year and got her a dozen cupcakes. She's obsessed with red velvet. She even has a red velvet colored couch in our living room. It's this crushed velvet material, really soft, but it's a magnet for cat hair. We don't even have a cat, but somehow there's always cat hair on it. I think it comes in on our clothes from outside, or maybe from the neighbors. The lady next door has three of those hairless cats. Sphynx cats, I think they're called? They look like little aliens, honestly. She puts them in little sweaters when it gets cold out. I saw one wearing a tiny argyle sweater vest the other day while she was taking out the recycling...\n\nBut yeah, no, I don't feel disconnected or in a daze. I'm usually paying attention to everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're observing yourself from outside your body?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you just ask me that? The outside the body thing? Um, yeah, still no. I'm definitely always just inside my own body. \n\nThough I guess I've been looking at myself a lot more lately because management just put in these massive floor-to-ceiling mirrors in the employee breakroom at work. I have no idea why they thought we needed those, it's just where we keep our coats and the time clock. The guy who installed them was this huge contractor named Gary, and he parked his giant truck right in the loading zone for like three hours. He ended up getting a parking ticket, and he was so mad he was actually screaming at the meter maid. I always feel really bad for meter maids, people are so incredibly mean to them. My cousin tried to do that job for a few months, but she quit because someone threw a half-empty large soda at her windshield. It was diet cola, so I guess at least it wasn't sticky, but still, who does that? She works at a bank now. She gets to sit in the air conditioning all day, which must be so nice. The AC at the restaurant has been making this awful rattling noise since Tuesday, and it's barely blowing any cold air. We had to prop the front doors open during the lunch rush just to get a breeze going... \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't observe myself from the outside.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid driving on the highway because of the anxiety it causes you?\n\nPatient: Driving on the highway? Oh, um, no. I don't avoid it. I actually don't mind the highway at all. I take it to work most days because the surface streets are just a nightmare with all the construction. \n\nThey've been tearing up Elm Street for like six months now, putting in some new water main or something. The orange cones are everywhere, and they just leave them out over the weekend even when nobody is working. It drives me crazy. My uncle used to work in construction, actually. He operated one of those huge yellow backhoes. He let me sit in the cab once when I was like ten, and it smelled so strongly of diesel fuel and old coffee. He always drank his coffee black, from this beaten-up metal thermos that had a dent in the side from when he dropped it off a scaffolding. He was always dropping things. He actually dropped his wedding ring down a storm drain on his anniversary, which was a whole disaster. My aunt made him sleep on the couch for a week, and he had to hire a guy from the city to come out with a special camera on a wire to try and find it. They never did, so he had to buy a new one, but he got a titanium one the second time because he said it was tougher. Titanium is supposed to be super light too, right? Like they use it for airplanes and stuff. I've always wanted to go on an airplane, but I haven't really had the money to take a vacation anywhere far away...\n\nBut yeah, the highway doesn't make me anxious or anything. I don't avoid it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not in control of your actions?\n\nPatient: Not in control? Like someone else is moving my arms or something? No, I don't think so. I'm the one doing things. I mean, I have to be, right? Especially carrying those huge trays at the restaurant. \n\nThey are so heavy, especially when someone orders the sizzling fajitas. The cast iron plates weigh a ton, and they come out popping and spitting grease everywhere. I actually got a tiny burn on my wrist last week from a stray drop of oil. It blistered up right away. I put some aloe on it from this plant my mom gave me. She has a whole sunroom full of plants, it's like a jungle in there. She talks to them, too, which I always thought was kind of funny. She says the ferns like classical music, but the succulents prefer jazz. My dad hates jazz. He says it just sounds like a bunch of musicians tuning their instruments at the same time. He's more of a classic rock guy, always playing his old vinyl records on this vintage turntable he bought at a garage sale. He spent like three weekends taking it apart and cleaning all the little gears and belts with a toothbrush. He used my old toothbrush, actually, the one I used to use for cleaning the grout in my bathroom. Grout is so hard to keep clean, especially in older apartments. Mine always gets this weird pinkish mold in the corners no matter how much bleach I use...\n\nBut no, I'm definitely in control of what I do. I don't feel like I'm not in control.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not really connected to what's happening around you?\n\nPatient: Connected to what's happening? Like, zoning out? Um, no, not really. I'm pretty tuned in to things. I kind of have to be, you know, working in a restaurant. You have to hear when the bell rings in the kitchen or the food gets cold. \n\nThe bell they use is actually super loud, it's one of those old-fashioned brass ones that you smack with your hand. The kitchen manager, Dave, hits it so hard sometimes I swear it's going to dent. Dave is always stressed out, though. He drinks like four energy drinks a shift. Those neon green ones that smell like battery acid. I tried one once when I was studying for finals back when I was taking classes at the community college. I was trying to get my associate's in hospitality, but I had to drop out because my car broke down and I couldn't afford to fix the transmission. Transmissions are ridiculously expensive. The mechanic I took it to had this huge German Shepherd that just wandered around the garage. His name was Buster, and he was so friendly, but he got grease all over my favorite pair of jeans when he leaned against me. I tried scrubbing them with dish soap and baking soda, but the stain never completely came out. It's a shame, too, because they were the perfect length. It is so hard to find jeans that aren't too long when you're kind of short. I usually have to hem them myself, which takes forever because I always lose my sewing needles...\n\nBut yeah, I don't really feel disconnected from things. I'm usually paying attention.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a dream or like things aren't real?\n\nPatient: Like things aren't real? Um, no, I don't think so. Things feel pretty real to me. I mean, waking up at 5 AM for the breakfast shift definitely feels real. \n\nThe alarm on my phone is this horrible blaring sound, like a submarine diving or something. I had to pick the most annoying one because otherwise I just sleep right through it. I actually slept through a fire alarm once in my old apartment building. It turned out it was just someone burning toast on the third floor, but the whole building had to evacuate into the parking lot. It was February, too, so it was freezing outside. My neighbor Mrs. Higgins came out in this giant pink fuzzy bathrobe and curlers in her hair. She looked like a giant flamingo. She used to bake the best snickerdoodle cookies, though. She'd bring me a plate every time she made a batch, and they were always perfectly soft in the middle. I tried asking for the recipe once, but she said it was a family secret that her grandmother brought over from Germany. I've always wanted to go to Germany, mostly just to see the castles. They look like they're straight out of a fairy tale, with the big towers and the moats. I wonder if they actually had alligators in the moats back then, or if that's just something they put in movies. I saw a movie last week about alligators, actually, one of those cheesy sci-fi ones where they mutate and get huge...\n\nBut no, I don't feel like I'm in a dream. Everything seems normal and real.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're watching a movie of yourself or your life, rather than actually being in it?\n\nPatient: Watching a movie of myself? No, I don't feel like that. I mean, I watch movies, but I'm definitely the one living my life. It's not a movie. Although, working at the restaurant can feel like a sitcom sometimes. \n\nJust yesterday, my coworker Sarah dropped a whole tray of water glasses. They shattered everywhere, right in the middle of the dinner rush. Sarah is always dropping things, she's so clumsy. I think it's because she wears these shoes that are way too big for her. She says they're orthopedic and help with her back pain, but they look like clown shoes. I used to have bad back pain when I worked retail, standing on those hard tile floors all day. I worked at this boutique that sold fancy soaps and candles. The smell in there was so overpowering, it gave me a headache by the end of every shift. Lavender and eucalyptus mixed together is not a good combination, let me tell you. My aunt used to make her own soap, actually. She'd use goat's milk and oatmeal. She lived on this little farm out in the country and had three goats. One of them was named Barnaby, and he would eat literally anything. He chewed a hole right through my favorite sweater once when I was visiting. It was a really nice wool sweater, too, the kind that's super itchy but really warm. I tried to patch it, but I'm terrible at sewing... \n\nBut no, I don't feel like I'm watching a movie of myself. I'm just living my life, you know?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Spaced out? Um, no, not really. I mean, I guess everyone gets a little distracted sometimes, but I wouldn't say I'm detached. I usually know what's going on. I try to stay focused, especially when it gets busy. 

Like on Sundays, the after-church crowd comes in and it is just wall-to-wall people. They always want extra ranch dressing. We go through gallons of ranch on Sundays. The prep cook, Luis, has to make it in these massive plastic buckets. He uses an immersion blender that looks like a weed whacker. My dad used to have a weed whacker that he'd use on the edges of our driveway every Saturday morning. It was so loud it would wake up the whole neighborhood. He was obsessed with having the perfect lawn. He even bought this special fertilizer that smelled like fish, and he'd spread it all over the grass. Our cat, Mittens, loved the smell of it and would roll around in the yard until she was covered in dirt and fertilizer. Then she'd try to come inside and sleep on the good sofa. My mom was so particular about that sofa. It had a floral pattern, like big pink cabbage roses, and she kept a plastic cover on it for years. It was the most uncomfortable thing to sit on, especially in the summer when you'd stick to the plastic. We didn't have central air conditioning back then, just this one window unit in the living room that rattled constantly. It sounded like there were marbles loose inside it...

But yeah, I don't feel detached or spaced out. I'm right here.

I'm the one doing things. I mean, I have to be, right? Especially carrying those huge trays at the restaurant. \n\nThey are so heavy, especially when someone orders the sizzling fajitas. The cast iron plates weigh a ton, and they come out popping and spitting grease everywhere. I actually got a tiny burn on my wrist last week from a stray drop of oil. It blistered up right away. It turned out it was just someone burning toast on the third floor, but the whole building had to evacuate into the parking lot. It was February, too, so it was freezing outside. My neighbor Mrs. Higgins came out in this giant pink fuzzy bathrobe and curlers in her hair. She looked like a giant flamingo. She used to bake the best snickerdoodle cookies, though. She'd bring me a plate every time she made a batch, and they were always perfectly soft in the middle. I tried asking for the recipe once, but she said it was a family secret that her grandmother brought over from Germany. I've always wanted to go to Germany, mostly just to see the castles. They look like they're straight out of a fairy tale, with the big towers and the moats. I wonder if they actually had alligators in the moats back then, or if that's just something they put in movies. I saw a movie last week about alligators, actually, one of those cheesy sci-fi ones where they mutate and get huge...\n\nBut no, I don't feel like I'm in a dream. Everything seems normal and real.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're watching a movie of yourself or your life, rather than actually being in it?\n\nPatient: Watching a movie of myself? No, I don't feel like that. I mean, I watch movies, but I'm definitely the one living my life. It's not a movie. Although, working at the restaurant can feel like a sitcom sometimes. \n\nJust yesterday, my coworker Sarah dropped a whole tray of water glasses. They shattered everywhere, right in the middle of the dinner rush. Sarah is always dropping things, she's so clumsy. I think it's because she wears these shoes that are way too big for her. She says they're orthopedic and help with her back pain, but they look like clown shoes. I used to have bad back pain when I worked retail, standing on those hard tile floors all day. I worked at this boutique that sold fancy soaps and candles. The smell in there was so overpowering, it gave me a headache by the end of every shift. Lavender and eucalyptus mixed together is not a good combination, let me tell you. My aunt used to make her own soap, actually. She'd use goat's milk and oatmeal. She lived on this little farm out in the country and had three goats. One of them was named Barnaby, and he would eat literally anything. He chewed a hole right through my favorite sweater once when I was visiting. It was a really nice wool sweater, too, the kind that's super itchy but really warm. I tried to patch it, but I'm terrible at sewing... \n\nBut no, I don't feel like I'm watching a movie of myself. I'm just living my life, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're spaced out or detached from yourself or your surroundings?\n\nPatient: Spaced out? Um, no, not really. I try to stay focused, especially when it gets busy. \n\nLike on Sundays, the after-church crowd comes in and it is just wall-to-wall people. They always want extra ranch dressing. It was the most uncomfortable thing to sit on, especially in the summer when you'd stick to the plastic. We didn't have central air conditioning back then, just this one window unit in the living room that rattled constantly. I just do my normal routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind and won't go away?\n\nPatient: Repeating thoughts? Um, no, I don't really have anything like that. My mind is pretty quiet, mostly. I mean, sometimes I get songs stuck in my head, like everyone else. Yesterday I had that old commercial jingle for the carpet cleaning place stuck in my head all morning. \n\nYou know the one, with the little animated bubbles that sing the phone number? I haven't even seen that commercial in years, but it just popped in there while I was wiping down the tables before we opened. We have to use this new sanitizer spray now, it smells exactly like artificial green apple. It's so strong, it kind of gives me a headache if I don't open the window in the back station. The window looks out at the alley, and there's this one stray cat that always sits on top of the blue dumpster. It's an orange tabby, missing half of its left ear. I started bringing little pieces of leftover salmon from the kitchen to leave out for it. The chef got kind of annoyed about it at first, but then he started saving the scraps for me in a little plastic deli container. He's actually a really nice guy, he just yells a lot during service. He used to work at this fancy French place downtown before he came to us, and he still wears those checkered chef pants even though our uniform is just black jeans... \n\nBut no, I don't have any thoughts that won't go away. Just normal stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body, watching yourself do things?\n\nPatient: Outside my body? Um, no, I don't think I've ever felt like that. That sounds kind of like a movie or something. I mostly just feel like I'm right here in my own skin. \n\nSpeaking of skin, I actually had to switch my laundry detergent recently because my arms were getting all itchy. I used to use the one with the lavender scent, but I guess they changed the formula or something. It's so annoying when companies do that. You find something you like, and then they just go and mess with it. Like my favorite brand of coffee. They completely redesigned the bag, and for like two weeks I couldn't find it on the shelf at the grocery store. I had to ask the stock boy, and he was so young, he looked like he was maybe sixteen. He had these bright blue braces on his teeth. I remember when my sister got braces, she cried for three days straight because her mouth hurt so much. She wouldn't eat anything except mashed potatoes and vanilla pudding. My mom got so tired of making mashed potatoes that she bought this huge industrial-sized box of the instant kind, the flakes? They taste so fake, like cardboard, but my sister didn't even care. She just wanted something she didn't have to chew. She still eats them sometimes when she's stressed out...\n\nBut no, I don't feel like I'm outside my body or watching myself. I'm just me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a daze or disconnected from what's happening around you?\n\nPatient: Disconnected? Like in a daze? Um, no, I don't really get like that. I'm usually pretty focused on what's going on around me, especially at work. You kind of have to be, you know? \n\nEspecially when it gets busy. Like last Friday, we had this massive party of twelve come in right at 7:00 without a reservation. They were celebrating a birthday, I think it was the grandfather's 80th or something. They brought in this huge sheet cake from that bakery down on 4th Street. The one with the pink awning? They make the best buttercream frosting. I actually went there for my roommate's birthday last year and got her a dozen cupcakes. She's obsessed with red velvet. She even has a red velvet colored couch in our living room. It's this crushed velvet material, really soft, but it's a magnet for cat hair. We don't even have a cat, but somehow there's always cat hair on it. I think it comes in on our clothes from outside, or maybe from the neighbors. The lady next door has three of those hairless cats. Sphynx cats, I think they're called? They look like little aliens, honestly. She puts them in little sweaters when it gets cold out. I saw one wearing a tiny argyle sweater vest the other day while she was taking out the recycling...\n\nBut yeah, no, I don't feel disconnected or in a daze. I'm usually paying attention to everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're observing yourself from outside your body?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you just ask me that? The outside the body thing? Um, yeah, still no. I'm definitely always just inside my own body. \n\nThough I guess I've been looking at myself a lot more lately because management just put in these massive floor-to-ceiling mirrors in the employee breakroom at work. I have no idea why they thought we needed those, it's just where we keep our coats and the time clock. The guy who installed them was this huge contractor named Gary, and he parked his giant truck right in the loading zone for like three hours. He ended up getting a parking ticket, and he was so mad he was actually screaming at the meter maid. I always feel really bad for meter maids, people are so incredibly mean to them. My cousin tried to do that job for a few months, but she quit because someone threw a half-empty large soda at her windshield. It was diet cola, so I guess at least it wasn't sticky, but still, who does that? She works at a bank now. She gets to sit in the air conditioning all day, which must be so nice. The AC at the restaurant has been making this awful rattling noise since Tuesday, and it's barely blowing any cold air. We had to prop the front doors open during the lunch rush just to get a breeze going... \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't observe myself from the outside.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid driving on the highway because of the anxiety it causes you?\n\nPatient: Driving on the highway? Oh, um, no. I don't avoid it. I actually don't mind the highway at all. I take it to work most days because the surface streets are just a nightmare with all the construction. \n\nThey've been tearing up Elm Street for like six months now, putting in some new water main or something. The orange cones are everywhere, and they just leave them out over the weekend even when nobody is working. It drives me crazy. My uncle used to work in construction, actually. He operated one of those huge yellow backhoes. He let me sit in the cab once when I was like ten, and it smelled so strongly of diesel fuel and old coffee. He always drank his coffee black, from this beaten-up metal thermos that had a dent in the side from when he dropped it off a scaffolding. He was always dropping things. He actually dropped his wedding ring down a storm drain on his anniversary, which was a whole disaster. My aunt made him sleep on the couch for a week, and he had to hire a guy from the city to come out with a special camera on a wire to try and find it. They never did, so he had to buy a new one, but he got a titanium one the second time because he said it was tougher. Titanium is supposed to be super light too, right? Like they use it for airplanes and stuff. I've always wanted to go on an airplane, but I haven't really had the money to take a vacation anywhere far away...\n\nBut yeah, the highway doesn't make me anxious or anything. I don't avoid it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not in control of your actions?\n\nPatient: Not in control? Like someone else is moving my arms or something? No, I don't think so. I'm the one doing things. I mean, I have to be, right? Especially carrying those huge trays at the restaurant. \n\nThey are so heavy, especially when someone orders the sizzling fajitas. The cast iron plates weigh a ton, and they come out popping and spitting grease everywhere. I actually got a tiny burn on my wrist last week from a stray drop of oil. It blistered up right away. I put some aloe on it from this plant my mom gave me. She has a whole sunroom full of plants, it's like a jungle in there. She talks to them, too, which I always thought was kind of funny. She says the ferns like classical music, but the succulents prefer jazz. My dad hates jazz. He says it just sounds like a bunch of musicians tuning their instruments at the same time. He's more of a classic rock guy, always playing his old vinyl records on this vintage turntable he bought at a garage sale. He spent like three weekends taking it apart and cleaning all the little gears and belts with a toothbrush. He used my old toothbrush, actually, the one I used to use for cleaning the grout in my bathroom. Grout is so hard to keep clean, especially in older apartments. Mine always gets this weird pinkish mold in the corners no matter how much bleach I use...\n\nBut no, I'm definitely in control of what I do. I don't feel like I'm not in control.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not really connected to what's happening around you?\n\nPatient: Connected to what's happening? Like, zoning out? Um, no, not really. I'm pretty tuned in to things. I kind of have to be, you know, working in a restaurant. You have to hear when the bell rings in the kitchen or the food gets cold. \n\nThe bell they use is actually super loud, it's one of those old-fashioned brass ones that you smack with your hand. The kitchen manager, Dave, hits it so hard sometimes I swear it's going to dent. Dave is always stressed out, though. He drinks like four energy drinks a shift. Those neon green ones that smell like battery acid. I tried one once when I was studying for finals back when I was taking classes at the community college. I was trying to get my associate's in hospitality, but I had to drop out because my car broke down and I couldn't afford to fix the transmission. Transmissions are ridiculously expensive. The mechanic I took it to had this huge German Shepherd that just wandered around the garage. His name was Buster, and he was so friendly, but he got grease all over my favorite pair of jeans when he leaned against me. I tried scrubbing them with dish soap and baking soda, but the stain never completely came out. It's a shame, too, because they were the perfect length. It is so hard to find jeans that aren't too long when you're kind of short. I usually have to hem them myself, which takes forever because I always lose my sewing needles...\n\nBut yeah, I don't really feel disconnected from things. I'm usually paying attention.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a dream or like things aren't real?\n\nPatient: Like things aren't real? Um, no, I don't think so. Things feel pretty real to me. I mean, waking up at 5 AM for the breakfast shift definitely feels real. \n\nThe alarm on my phone is this horrible blaring sound, like a submarine diving or something. I had to pick the most annoying one because otherwise I just sleep right through it. I actually slept through a fire alarm once in my old apartment building. It turned out it was just someone burning toast on the third floor, but the whole building had to evacuate into the parking lot. It was February, too, so it was freezing outside. My neighbor Mrs. Higgins came out in this giant pink fuzzy bathrobe and curlers in her hair. She looked like a giant flamingo. She used to bake the best snickerdoodle cookies, though. She'd bring me a plate every time she made a batch, and they were always perfectly soft in the middle. I tried asking for the recipe once, but she said it was a family secret that her grandmother brought over from Germany. I've always wanted to go to Germany, mostly just to see the castles. They look like they're straight out of a fairy tale, with the big towers and the moats. I wonder if they actually had alligators in the moats back then, or if that's just something they put in movies. I saw a movie last week about alligators, actually, one of those cheesy sci-fi ones where they mutate and get huge...\n\nBut no, I don't feel like I'm in a dream. Everything seems normal and real.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're watching a movie of yourself or your life, rather than actually being in it?\n\nPatient: Watching a movie of myself? No, I don't feel like that. I mean, I watch movies, but I'm definitely the one living my life. It's not a movie. Although, working at the restaurant can feel like a sitcom sometimes. \n\nJust yesterday, my coworker Sarah dropped a whole tray of water glasses. They shattered everywhere, right in the middle of the dinner rush. Sarah is always dropping things, she's so clumsy. I think it's because she wears these shoes that are way too big for her. She says they're orthopedic and help with her back pain, but they look like clown shoes. I used to have bad back pain when I worked retail, standing on those hard tile floors all day. I worked at this boutique that sold fancy soaps and candles. The smell in there was so overpowering, it gave me a headache by the end of every shift. Lavender and eucalyptus mixed together is not a good combination, let me tell you. My aunt used to make her own soap, actually. She'd use goat's milk and oatmeal. She lived on this little farm out in the country and had three goats. One of them was named Barnaby, and he would eat literally anything. He chewed a hole right through my favorite sweater once when I was visiting. It was a really nice wool sweater, too, the kind that's super itchy but really warm. I tried to patch it, but I'm terrible at sewing... \n\nBut no, I don't feel like I'm watching a movie of myself. I'm just living my life, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're spaced out or detached from yourself or your surroundings?\n\nPatient: Spaced out? Um, no, not really. I mean, I guess everyone gets a little distracted sometimes, but I wouldn't say I'm detached. I usually know what's going on. I try to stay focused, especially when it gets busy. \n\nLike on Sundays, the after-church crowd comes in and it is just wall-to-wall people. They always want extra ranch dressing. We go through gallons of ranch on Sundays. The prep cook, Luis, has to make it in these massive plastic buckets. He uses an immersion blender that looks like a weed whacker. My dad used to have a weed whacker that he'd use on the edges of our driveway every Saturday morning. It was so loud it would wake up the whole neighborhood. He was obsessed with having the perfect lawn. He even bought this special fertilizer that smelled like fish, and he'd spread it all over the grass. Our cat, Mittens, loved the smell of it and would roll around in the yard until she was covered in dirt and fertilizer. Then she'd try to come inside and sleep on the good sofa. My mom was so particular about that sofa. It had a floral pattern, like big pink cabbage roses, and she kept a plastic cover on it for years. It was the most uncomfortable thing to sit on, especially in the summer when you'd stick to the plastic. We didn't have central air conditioning back then, just this one window unit in the living room that rattled constantly. It sounded like there were marbles loose inside it...\n\nBut yeah, I don't feel detached or spaced out. I'm right here.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not myself? Um, no, I don't think so. I mean, I'm always just me. Although, it was kind of funny last Tuesday because I actually lost my name tag at work. It must have fallen off in the walk-in cooler, or maybe out in the parking lot when I was taking the trash out. So my manager made me wear a spare one from the office, and it said "Brenda" on it. 

All day, customers kept calling me Brenda. "Can I get some more coffee, Brenda?" "Check please, Brenda." It was so weird. The only Brenda I actually know is my mom's cousin, and she lives way out in Ohio. She collects those little porcelain dolls, you know the ones with the big glassy eyes and the painted-on eyelashes? She has a whole glass cabinet full of them in her living room. Whenever we'd visit when I was little, I was terrified of them. I always felt like they were watching me while I ate my macaroni and cheese. My mom made the absolute best macaroni and cheese, baked in the oven with the crispy breadcrumbs on top. She'd use three different kinds of cheese—sharp cheddar, gruyere, and I think parmesan. We'd always have it for Thanksgiving instead of stuffing because my younger brother was allergic to celery, and you really can't make good traditional stuffing without celery. He used to break out in hives if he even touched a piece of it. We had to rush him to the emergency room once when he was seven because he ate some potato salad at a church picnic that had hidden celery in it... 

But anyway, no, I always feel like myself. I'm definitely not Brenda, but I'm me. Everything feels fine with who I am.

Everything seems normal and real.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're watching a movie of yourself or your life, rather than actually being in it?\n\nPatient: Watching a movie of myself? No, I don't feel like that. I mean, I watch movies, but I'm definitely the one living my life. It's not a movie. Although, working at the restaurant can feel like a sitcom sometimes. \n\nJust yesterday, my coworker Sarah dropped a whole tray of water glasses. They shattered everywhere, right in the middle of the dinner rush. Sarah is always dropping things, she's so clumsy. I think it's because she wears these shoes that are way too big for her. She says they're orthopedic and help with her back pain, but they look like clown shoes. I used to have bad back pain when I worked retail, standing on those hard tile floors all day. I worked at this boutique that sold fancy soaps and candles. The smell in there was so overpowering, it gave me a headache by the end of every shift. Lavender and eucalyptus mixed together is not a good combination, let me tell you. My aunt used to make her own soap, actually. She'd use goat's milk and oatmeal. She lived on this little farm out in the country and had three goats. One of them was named Barnaby, and he would eat literally anything. He chewed a hole right through my favorite sweater once when I was visiting. It was a really nice wool sweater, too, the kind that's super itchy but really warm. I'm just living my life, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're spaced out or detached from yourself or your surroundings?\n\nPatient: Spaced out? Um, no, not really. We go through gallons of ranch on Sundays. The prep cook, Luis, has to make it in these massive plastic buckets. He uses an immersion blender that looks like a weed whacker. My dad used to have a weed whacker that he'd use on the edges of our driveway every Saturday morning. It was so loud it would wake up the whole neighborhood. He was obsessed with having the perfect lawn. He even bought this special fertilizer that smelled like fish, and he'd spread it all over the grass. Our cat, Mittens, loved the smell of it and would roll around in the yard until she was covered in dirt and fertilizer. Then she'd try to come inside and sleep on the good sofa. My mom was so particular about that sofa. It had a floral pattern, like big pink cabbage roses, and she kept a plastic cover on it for years. It was the most uncomfortable thing to sit on, especially in the summer when you'd stick to the plastic. We didn't have central air conditioning back then, just this one window unit in the living room that rattled constantly. It sounded like there were marbles loose inside it...\n\nBut yeah, I don't feel detached or spaced out. I'm right here.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not yourself or like something is off about your sense of self?\n\nPatient: Not myself? Um, no, I don't think so. I mean, I'm always just me. He used to break out in hives if he even touched a piece of it. Anyway, the movie was about this dog that waits for its owner at a train station for years and years after the owner dies. I was bawling by the end of it. It's crazy how loyal dogs are, you know? Cats would never do that. My aunt had a cat that would literally hide under the couch and swipe at your ankles if you walked by. She called him Mr. Whiskers, which was such a cute name for such a mean cat. She ended up having to give him to a farm because he scratched the mailman. The mailman was so mad, he threatened to stop delivering their mail entirely...\n\nDoctor: Do you tend to avoid certain situations or tasks because you're worried about freezing up like you do at work?\n\nPatient: Avoid things? Um, no, I don't really think so. I mean, I still go to my shifts at the restaurant and do whatever I'm supposed to do. I mostly work the dinner rush anyway. We actually just changed our menu for the summer, which is always kind of a pain because we have to memorize all the new seasonal specials. \n\nThey added this peach and goat cheese salad thing. Peaches are really good right now, actually. I bought a whole basket of them at the farmer's market on Saturday. The guy selling them was wearing this huge straw hat, kind of like a sombrero, but made of woven grass or something. He said he grew them all in his own orchard down south. I tried to grow cherry tomatoes on my apartment balcony once, but the birds kept eating them before they even turned red. Pigeons are just so bold in the city, they'll walk right up to your feet. One time a pigeon actually walked right into the restaurant because the host left the front door propped open to get a breeze, and the manager was running around with a broom trying to shoo it back out. It knocked over a whole tray of water glasses... \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't avoid doing things or anything like that. I just do my normal routine.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind and won't go away?\n\nPatient: Repeating thoughts? Um, no, I don't really have anything like that. My mind is pretty quiet, mostly. I mean, sometimes I get songs stuck in my head, like everyone else. Yesterday I had that old commercial jingle for the carpet cleaning place stuck in my head all morning. \n\nYou know the one, with the little animated bubbles that sing the phone number? I haven't even seen that commercial in years, but it just popped in there while I was wiping down the tables before we opened. We have to use this new sanitizer spray now, it smells exactly like artificial green apple. It's so strong, it kind of gives me a headache if I don't open the window in the back station. The window looks out at the alley, and there's this one stray cat that always sits on top of the blue dumpster. It's an orange tabby, missing half of its left ear. I started bringing little pieces of leftover salmon from the kitchen to leave out for it. The chef got kind of annoyed about it at first, but then he started saving the scraps for me in a little plastic deli container. He's actually a really nice guy, he just yells a lot during service. He used to work at this fancy French place downtown before he came to us, and he still wears those checkered chef pants even though our uniform is just black jeans... \n\nBut no, I don't have any thoughts that won't go away. Just normal stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body, watching yourself do things?\n\nPatient: Outside my body? Um, no, I don't think I've ever felt like that. That sounds kind of like a movie or something. I mostly just feel like I'm right here in my own skin. \n\nSpeaking of skin, I actually had to switch my laundry detergent recently because my arms were getting all itchy. I used to use the one with the lavender scent, but I guess they changed the formula or something. It's so annoying when companies do that. You find something you like, and then they just go and mess with it. Like my favorite brand of coffee. They completely redesigned the bag, and for like two weeks I couldn't find it on the shelf at the grocery store. I had to ask the stock boy, and he was so young, he looked like he was maybe sixteen. He had these bright blue braces on his teeth. I remember when my sister got braces, she cried for three days straight because her mouth hurt so much. She wouldn't eat anything except mashed potatoes and vanilla pudding. My mom got so tired of making mashed potatoes that she bought this huge industrial-sized box of the instant kind, the flakes? They taste so fake, like cardboard, but my sister didn't even care. She just wanted something she didn't have to chew. She still eats them sometimes when she's stressed out...\n\nBut no, I don't feel like I'm outside my body or watching myself. I'm just me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a daze or disconnected from what's happening around you?\n\nPatient: Disconnected? Like in a daze? Um, no, I don't really get like that. I'm usually pretty focused on what's going on around me, especially at work. You kind of have to be, you know? \n\nEspecially when it gets busy. Like last Friday, we had this massive party of twelve come in right at 7:00 without a reservation. They were celebrating a birthday, I think it was the grandfather's 80th or something. They brought in this huge sheet cake from that bakery down on 4th Street. The one with the pink awning? They make the best buttercream frosting. I actually went there for my roommate's birthday last year and got her a dozen cupcakes. She's obsessed with red velvet. She even has a red velvet colored couch in our living room. It's this crushed velvet material, really soft, but it's a magnet for cat hair. We don't even have a cat, but somehow there's always cat hair on it. I think it comes in on our clothes from outside, or maybe from the neighbors. The lady next door has three of those hairless cats. Sphynx cats, I think they're called? They look like little aliens, honestly. She puts them in little sweaters when it gets cold out. I saw one wearing a tiny argyle sweater vest the other day while she was taking out the recycling...\n\nBut yeah, no, I don't feel disconnected or in a daze. I'm usually paying attention to everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're observing yourself from outside your body?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you just ask me that? The outside the body thing? Um, yeah, still no. I'm definitely always just inside my own body. \n\nThough I guess I've been looking at myself a lot more lately because management just put in these massive floor-to-ceiling mirrors in the employee breakroom at work. I have no idea why they thought we needed those, it's just where we keep our coats and the time clock. The guy who installed them was this huge contractor named Gary, and he parked his giant truck right in the loading zone for like three hours. He ended up getting a parking ticket, and he was so mad he was actually screaming at the meter maid. I always feel really bad for meter maids, people are so incredibly mean to them. My cousin tried to do that job for a few months, but she quit because someone threw a half-empty large soda at her windshield. It was diet cola, so I guess at least it wasn't sticky, but still, who does that? She works at a bank now. She gets to sit in the air conditioning all day, which must be so nice. The AC at the restaurant has been making this awful rattling noise since Tuesday, and it's barely blowing any cold air. We had to prop the front doors open during the lunch rush just to get a breeze going... \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't observe myself from the outside.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid driving on the highway because of the anxiety it causes you?\n\nPatient: Driving on the highway? Oh, um, no. I don't avoid it. I actually don't mind the highway at all. I take it to work most days because the surface streets are just a nightmare with all the construction. \n\nThey've been tearing up Elm Street for like six months now, putting in some new water main or something. The orange cones are everywhere, and they just leave them out over the weekend even when nobody is working. It drives me crazy. My uncle used to work in construction, actually. He operated one of those huge yellow backhoes. He let me sit in the cab once when I was like ten, and it smelled so strongly of diesel fuel and old coffee. He always drank his coffee black, from this beaten-up metal thermos that had a dent in the side from when he dropped it off a scaffolding. He was always dropping things. He actually dropped his wedding ring down a storm drain on his anniversary, which was a whole disaster. My aunt made him sleep on the couch for a week, and he had to hire a guy from the city to come out with a special camera on a wire to try and find it. They never did, so he had to buy a new one, but he got a titanium one the second time because he said it was tougher. Titanium is supposed to be super light too, right? Like they use it for airplanes and stuff. I've always wanted to go on an airplane, but I haven't really had the money to take a vacation anywhere far away...\n\nBut yeah, the highway doesn't make me anxious or anything. I don't avoid it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not in control of your actions?\n\nPatient: Not in control? Like someone else is moving my arms or something? No, I don't think so. I'm the one doing things. I mean, I have to be, right? Especially carrying those huge trays at the restaurant. \n\nThey are so heavy, especially when someone orders the sizzling fajitas. The cast iron plates weigh a ton, and they come out popping and spitting grease everywhere. I actually got a tiny burn on my wrist last week from a stray drop of oil. It blistered up right away. I put some aloe on it from this plant my mom gave me. She has a whole sunroom full of plants, it's like a jungle in there. She talks to them, too, which I always thought was kind of funny. She says the ferns like classical music, but the succulents prefer jazz. My dad hates jazz. He says it just sounds like a bunch of musicians tuning their instruments at the same time. He's more of a classic rock guy, always playing his old vinyl records on this vintage turntable he bought at a garage sale. He spent like three weekends taking it apart and cleaning all the little gears and belts with a toothbrush. He used my old toothbrush, actually, the one I used to use for cleaning the grout in my bathroom. Grout is so hard to keep clean, especially in older apartments. Mine always gets this weird pinkish mold in the corners no matter how much bleach I use...\n\nBut no, I'm definitely in control of what I do. I don't feel like I'm not in control.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not really connected to what's happening around you?\n\nPatient: Connected to what's happening? Like, zoning out? Um, no, not really. I'm pretty tuned in to things. I kind of have to be, you know, working in a restaurant. You have to hear when the bell rings in the kitchen or the food gets cold. \n\nThe bell they use is actually super loud, it's one of those old-fashioned brass ones that you smack with your hand. The kitchen manager, Dave, hits it so hard sometimes I swear it's going to dent. Dave is always stressed out, though. He drinks like four energy drinks a shift. Those neon green ones that smell like battery acid. I tried one once when I was studying for finals back when I was taking classes at the community college. I was trying to get my associate's in hospitality, but I had to drop out because my car broke down and I couldn't afford to fix the transmission. Transmissions are ridiculously expensive. The mechanic I took it to had this huge German Shepherd that just wandered around the garage. His name was Buster, and he was so friendly, but he got grease all over my favorite pair of jeans when he leaned against me. I tried scrubbing them with dish soap and baking soda, but the stain never completely came out. It's a shame, too, because they were the perfect length. It is so hard to find jeans that aren't too long when you're kind of short. I usually have to hem them myself, which takes forever because I always lose my sewing needles...\n\nBut yeah, I don't really feel disconnected from things. I'm usually paying attention.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a dream or like things aren't real?\n\nPatient: Like things aren't real? Um, no, I don't think so. Things feel pretty real to me. I mean, waking up at 5 AM for the breakfast shift definitely feels real. \n\nThe alarm on my phone is this horrible blaring sound, like a submarine diving or something. I had to pick the most annoying one because otherwise I just sleep right through it. I actually slept through a fire alarm once in my old apartment building. It turned out it was just someone burning toast on the third floor, but the whole building had to evacuate into the parking lot. It was February, too, so it was freezing outside. My neighbor Mrs. Higgins came out in this giant pink fuzzy bathrobe and curlers in her hair. She looked like a giant flamingo. She used to bake the best snickerdoodle cookies, though. She'd bring me a plate every time she made a batch, and they were always perfectly soft in the middle. I tried asking for the recipe once, but she said it was a family secret that her grandmother brought over from Germany. I've always wanted to go to Germany, mostly just to see the castles. They look like they're straight out of a fairy tale, with the big towers and the moats. I wonder if they actually had alligators in the moats back then, or if that's just something they put in movies. I saw a movie last week about alligators, actually, one of those cheesy sci-fi ones where they mutate and get huge...\n\nBut no, I don't feel like I'm in a dream. Everything seems normal and real.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're watching a movie of yourself or your life, rather than actually being in it?\n\nPatient: Watching a movie of myself? No, I don't feel like that. I mean, I watch movies, but I'm definitely the one living my life. It's not a movie. Although, working at the restaurant can feel like a sitcom sometimes. \n\nJust yesterday, my coworker Sarah dropped a whole tray of water glasses. They shattered everywhere, right in the middle of the dinner rush. Sarah is always dropping things, she's so clumsy. I think it's because she wears these shoes that are way too big for her. She says they're orthopedic and help with her back pain, but they look like clown shoes. I used to have bad back pain when I worked retail, standing on those hard tile floors all day. I worked at this boutique that sold fancy soaps and candles. The smell in there was so overpowering, it gave me a headache by the end of every shift. Lavender and eucalyptus mixed together is not a good combination, let me tell you. My aunt used to make her own soap, actually. She'd use goat's milk and oatmeal. She lived on this little farm out in the country and had three goats. One of them was named Barnaby, and he would eat literally anything. He chewed a hole right through my favorite sweater once when I was visiting. It was a really nice wool sweater, too, the kind that's super itchy but really warm. I tried to patch it, but I'm terrible at sewing... \n\nBut no, I don't feel like I'm watching a movie of myself. I'm just living my life, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're spaced out or detached from yourself or your surroundings?\n\nPatient: Spaced out? Um, no, not really. I mean, I guess everyone gets a little distracted sometimes, but I wouldn't say I'm detached. I usually know what's going on. I try to stay focused, especially when it gets busy. \n\nLike on Sundays, the after-church crowd comes in and it is just wall-to-wall people. They always want extra ranch dressing. We go through gallons of ranch on Sundays. The prep cook, Luis, has to make it in these massive plastic buckets. He uses an immersion blender that looks like a weed whacker. My dad used to have a weed whacker that he'd use on the edges of our driveway every Saturday morning. It was so loud it would wake up the whole neighborhood. He was obsessed with having the perfect lawn. He even bought this special fertilizer that smelled like fish, and he'd spread it all over the grass. Our cat, Mittens, loved the smell of it and would roll around in the yard until she was covered in dirt and fertilizer. Then she'd try to come inside and sleep on the good sofa. My mom was so particular about that sofa. It had a floral pattern, like big pink cabbage roses, and she kept a plastic cover on it for years. It was the most uncomfortable thing to sit on, especially in the summer when you'd stick to the plastic. We didn't have central air conditioning back then, just this one window unit in the living room that rattled constantly. It sounded like there were marbles loose inside it...\n\nBut yeah, I don't feel detached or spaced out. I'm right here.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not yourself or like something is off about your sense of self?\n\nPatient: Not myself? Um, no, I don't think so. I mean, I'm always just me. Although, it was kind of funny last Tuesday because I actually lost my name tag at work. It must have fallen off in the walk-in cooler, or maybe out in the parking lot when I was taking the trash out. So my manager made me wear a spare one from the office, and it said \"Brenda\" on it. \n\nAll day, customers kept calling me Brenda. \"Can I get some more coffee, Brenda?\" \"Check please, Brenda.\" It was so weird. The only Brenda I actually know is my mom's cousin, and she lives way out in Ohio. She collects those little porcelain dolls, you know the ones with the big glassy eyes and the painted-on eyelashes? She has a whole glass cabinet full of them in her living room. Whenever we'd visit when I was little, I was terrified of them. I always felt like they were watching me while I ate my macaroni and cheese. My mom made the absolute best macaroni and cheese, baked in the oven with the crispy breadcrumbs on top. She'd use three different kinds of cheese—sharp cheddar, gruyere, and I think parmesan. We'd always have it for Thanksgiving instead of stuffing because my younger brother was allergic to celery, and you really can't make good traditional stuffing without celery. He used to break out in hives if he even touched a piece of it. We had to rush him to the emergency room once when he was seven because he ate some potato salad at a church picnic that had hidden celery in it... \n\nBut anyway, no, I always feel like myself. I'm definitely not Brenda, but I'm me. Everything feels fine with who I am.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Our cat, Mittens, loved the smell of it and would roll around in the yard until she was covered in dirt and fertilizer. Then she'd try to come inside and sleep on the good sofa. My mom was so particular about that sofa. It had a floral pattern, like big pink cabbage roses, and she kept a plastic cover on it for years. It was the most uncomfortable thing to sit on, especially in the summer when you'd stick to the plastic. We didn't have central air conditioning back then, just this one window unit in the living room that rattled constantly. It sounded like there were marbles loose inside it...\n\nBut yeah, I don't feel detached or spaced out. I'm right here.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not yourself or like something is off about your sense of self?\n\nPatient: Not myself? Um, no, I don't think so. I mean, I'm always just me. Although, it was kind of funny last Tuesday because I actually lost my name tag at work. It must have fallen off in the walk-in cooler, or maybe out in the parking lot when I was taking the trash out. So my manager made me wear a spare one from the office, and it said \"Brenda\" on it. \n\nAll day, customers kept calling me Brenda. \"Can I get some more coffee, Brenda?\" \"Check please, Brenda.\" It was so weird. The only Brenda I actually know is my mom's cousin, and she lives way out in Ohio. She collects those little porcelain dolls, you know the ones with the big glassy eyes and the painted-on eyelashes? She has a whole glass cabinet full of them in her living room. Whenever we'd visit when I was little, I was terrified of them. I always felt like they were watching me while I ate my macaroni and cheese. My mom made the absolute best macaroni and cheese, baked in the oven with the crispy breadcrumbs on top. She'd use three different kinds of cheese—sharp cheddar, gruyere, and I think parmesan. We'd always have it for Thanksgiving instead of stuffing because my younger brother was allergic to celery, and you really can't make good traditional stuffing without celery. He used to break out in hives if he even touched a piece of it. We had to rush him to the emergency room once when he was seven because he ate some potato salad at a church picnic that had hidden celery in it... \n\nBut anyway, no, I always feel like myself. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Going through the motions? I mean, I guess work can feel a little repetitive sometimes, but I wouldn't say I'm not living my life. I'm definitely living it. Although, rolling silverware at the end of a shift is the definition of going through the motions. You just stand there in the back alley by the dish pit, folding napkin after napkin. 

The napkins we use are this really stiff linen, and they're supposed to be white, but they're all kind of a dingy gray color now from being washed so many times. The laundry service picks them up on Tuesdays and Thursdays. The guy who drives the laundry truck, his name is Dave, and he always brings his dog with him. It's a pug named Waffles. Waffles has this little underbite that makes him look like he's always smiling, but he breathes so loud, like a tiny little freight train. Pugs always have those breathing problems, you know? My neighbor growing up had a pug, and also a parrot. An African Grey. That bird was so smart, it learned how to mimic the sound of the microwave beeping. So my neighbor would always be running into the kitchen thinking her tea was done, but it was just the bird. The bird also learned how to cough exactly like her husband, who smoked a pack a day. He smoked those long, skinny cigarettes, the ones that come in a soft pack. He'd always leave the empty packs on the porch railing, and the wind would blow them into our yard. My mom would get so mad, she'd go out there with a broom and sweep them right back over the property line...

But yeah, no, I'm definitely living my life. I'm not just going through the motions or anything.

The orange cones are everywhere, and they just leave them out over the weekend even when nobody is working. It drives me crazy. My uncle used to work in construction, actually. He always drank his coffee black, from this beaten-up metal thermos that had a dent in the side from when he dropped it off a scaffolding. He was always dropping things. I don't avoid it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not in control of your actions?\n\nPatient: Not in control? Like someone else is moving my arms or something? No, I don't think so. I put some aloe on it from this plant my mom gave me. She has a whole sunroom full of plants, it's like a jungle in there. She talks to them, too, which I always thought was kind of funny. She says the ferns like classical music, but the succulents prefer jazz. My dad hates jazz. He says it just sounds like a bunch of musicians tuning their instruments at the same time. He's more of a classic rock guy, always playing his old vinyl records on this vintage turntable he bought at a garage sale. He spent like three weekends taking it apart and cleaning all the little gears and belts with a toothbrush. Mine always gets this weird pinkish mold in the corners no matter how much bleach I use...\n\nBut no, I'm definitely in control of what I do. I don't feel like I'm not in control.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not really connected to what's happening around you?\n\nPatient: Connected to what's happening? Like, zoning out? It is so hard to find jeans that aren't too long when you're kind of short. I usually have to hem them myself, which takes forever because I always lose my sewing needles...\n\nBut yeah, I don't really feel disconnected from things. I'm usually paying attention.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're in a dream or like things aren't real?\n\nPatient: Like things aren't real? Um, no, I don't think so. Things feel pretty real to me. I mean, waking up at 5 AM for the breakfast shift definitely feels real. \n\nThe alarm on my phone is this horrible blaring sound, like a submarine diving or something. I had to pick the most annoying one because otherwise I just sleep right through it. I actually slept through a fire alarm once in my old apartment building. It turned out it was just someone burning toast on the third floor, but the whole building had to evacuate into the parking lot. It was February, too, so it was freezing outside. My neighbor Mrs. Higgins came out in this giant pink fuzzy bathrobe and curlers in her hair. She looked like a giant flamingo. She used to bake the best snickerdoodle cookies, though. She'd bring me a plate every time she made a batch, and they were always perfectly soft in the middle. I tried asking for the recipe once, but she said it was a family secret that her grandmother brought over from Germany. I've always wanted to go to Germany, mostly just to see the castles. They look like they're straight out of a fairy tale, with the big towers and the moats. I wonder if they actually had alligators in the moats back then, or if that's just something they put in movies. I saw a movie last week about alligators, actually, one of those cheesy sci-fi ones where they mutate and get huge...\n\nBut no, I don't feel like I'm in a dream. Everything seems normal and real.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're watching a movie of yourself or your life, rather than actually being in it?\n\nPatient: Watching a movie of myself? No, I don't feel like that. I mean, I watch movies, but I'm definitely the one living my life. It's not a movie. Although, working at the restaurant can feel like a sitcom sometimes. \n\nJust yesterday, my coworker Sarah dropped a whole tray of water glasses. They shattered everywhere, right in the middle of the dinner rush. Sarah is always dropping things, she's so clumsy. I think it's because she wears these shoes that are way too big for her. She says they're orthopedic and help with her back pain, but they look like clown shoes. I used to have bad back pain when I worked retail, standing on those hard tile floors all day. I worked at this boutique that sold fancy soaps and candles. The smell in there was so overpowering, it gave me a headache by the end of every shift. Lavender and eucalyptus mixed together is not a good combination, let me tell you. My aunt used to make her own soap, actually. She'd use goat's milk and oatmeal. She lived on this little farm out in the country and had three goats. One of them was named Barnaby, and he would eat literally anything. He chewed a hole right through my favorite sweater once when I was visiting. It was a really nice wool sweater, too, the kind that's super itchy but really warm. I tried to patch it, but I'm terrible at sewing... \n\nBut no, I don't feel like I'm watching a movie of myself. I'm just living my life, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're spaced out or detached from yourself or your surroundings?\n\nPatient: Spaced out? Um, no, not really. I mean, I guess everyone gets a little distracted sometimes, but I wouldn't say I'm detached. I usually know what's going on. I try to stay focused, especially when it gets busy. \n\nLike on Sundays, the after-church crowd comes in and it is just wall-to-wall people. They always want extra ranch dressing. We go through gallons of ranch on Sundays. The prep cook, Luis, has to make it in these massive plastic buckets. He uses an immersion blender that looks like a weed whacker. My dad used to have a weed whacker that he'd use on the edges of our driveway every Saturday morning. It was so loud it would wake up the whole neighborhood. He was obsessed with having the perfect lawn. He even bought this special fertilizer that smelled like fish, and he'd spread it all over the grass. Our cat, Mittens, loved the smell of it and would roll around in the yard until she was covered in dirt and fertilizer. Then she'd try to come inside and sleep on the good sofa. My mom was so particular about that sofa. It had a floral pattern, like big pink cabbage roses, and she kept a plastic cover on it for years. It was the most uncomfortable thing to sit on, especially in the summer when you'd stick to the plastic. We didn't have central air conditioning back then, just this one window unit in the living room that rattled constantly. It sounded like there were marbles loose inside it...\n\nBut yeah, I don't feel detached or spaced out. I'm right here.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not yourself or like something is off about your sense of self?\n\nPatient: Not myself? Um, no, I don't think so. I mean, I'm always just me. Although, it was kind of funny last Tuesday because I actually lost my name tag at work. It must have fallen off in the walk-in cooler, or maybe out in the parking lot when I was taking the trash out. So my manager made me wear a spare one from the office, and it said \"Brenda\" on it. \n\nAll day, customers kept calling me Brenda. \"Can I get some more coffee, Brenda?\" \"Check please, Brenda.\" It was so weird. The only Brenda I actually know is my mom's cousin, and she lives way out in Ohio. She collects those little porcelain dolls, you know the ones with the big glassy eyes and the painted-on eyelashes? She has a whole glass cabinet full of them in her living room. Whenever we'd visit when I was little, I was terrified of them. I always felt like they were watching me while I ate my macaroni and cheese. My mom made the absolute best macaroni and cheese, baked in the oven with the crispy breadcrumbs on top. She'd use three different kinds of cheese—sharp cheddar, gruyere, and I think parmesan. We'd always have it for Thanksgiving instead of stuffing because my younger brother was allergic to celery, and you really can't make good traditional stuffing without celery. He used to break out in hives if he even touched a piece of it. We had to rush him to the emergency room once when he was seven because he ate some potato salad at a church picnic that had hidden celery in it... \n\nBut anyway, no, I always feel like myself. I'm definitely not Brenda, but I'm me. Everything feels fine with who I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not really living your life, but rather just going through the motions?\n\nPatient: Going through the motions? I mean, I guess work can feel a little repetitive sometimes, but I wouldn't say I'm not living my life. I'm definitely living it. Although, rolling silverware at the end of a shift is the definition of going through the motions. You just stand there in the back alley by the dish pit, folding napkin after napkin. \n\nThe napkins we use are this really stiff linen, and they're supposed to be white, but they're all kind of a dingy gray color now from being washed so many times. The laundry service picks them up on Tuesdays and Thursdays. The guy who drives the laundry truck, his name is Dave, and he always brings his dog with him. It's a pug named Waffles. Waffles has this little underbite that makes him look like he's always smiling, but he breathes so loud, like a tiny little freight train. Pugs always have those breathing problems, you know? My neighbor growing up had a pug, and also a parrot. An African Grey. That bird was so smart, it learned how to mimic the sound of the microwave beeping. So my neighbor would always be running into the kitchen thinking her tea was done, but it was just the bird. The bird also learned how to cough exactly like her husband, who smoked a pack a day. He smoked those long, skinny cigarettes, the ones that come in a soft pack. He'd always leave the empty packs on the porch railing, and the wind would blow them into our yard. My mom would get so mad, she'd go out there with a broom and sweep them right back over the property line...\n\nBut yeah, no, I'm definitely living my life. I'm not just going through the motions or anything.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Not a part of it? Um, no, I'm usually right in the middle of everything. I mean, at the restaurant, you kind of have to be. Like last Friday, we had this massive 50th birthday party in the back section, and I was definitely part of the chaos. They had goats there, too, and one time a goat tried to eat my shoelaces...

I tried to patch it, but I'm terrible at sewing... \n\nBut no, I don't feel like I'm watching a movie of myself. I'm just living my life, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're spaced out or detached from yourself or your surroundings?\n\nPatient: Spaced out? Um, no, not really. I mean, I guess everyone gets a little distracted sometimes, but I wouldn't say I'm detached. I usually know what's going on. I try to stay focused, especially when it gets busy. \n\nLike on Sundays, the after-church crowd comes in and it is just wall-to-wall people. They always want extra ranch dressing. We go through gallons of ranch on Sundays. The prep cook, Luis, has to make it in these massive plastic buckets. He uses an immersion blender that looks like a weed whacker. My dad used to have a weed whacker that he'd use on the edges of our driveway every Saturday morning. It was so loud it would wake up the whole neighborhood. He was obsessed with having the perfect lawn. He even bought this special fertilizer that smelled like fish, and he'd spread it all over the grass. Our cat, Mittens, loved the smell of it and would roll around in the yard until she was covered in dirt and fertilizer. Then she'd try to come inside and sleep on the good sofa. My mom was so particular about that sofa. It had a floral pattern, like big pink cabbage roses, and she kept a plastic cover on it for years. It was the most uncomfortable thing to sit on, especially in the summer when you'd stick to the plastic. We didn't have central air conditioning back then, just this one window unit in the living room that rattled constantly. It sounded like there were marbles loose inside it...\n\nBut yeah, I don't feel detached or spaced out. I'm right here.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not yourself or like something is off about your sense of self?\n\nPatient: Not myself? Um, no, I don't think so. I mean, I'm always just me. Although, it was kind of funny last Tuesday because I actually lost my name tag at work. It must have fallen off in the walk-in cooler, or maybe out in the parking lot when I was taking the trash out. So my manager made me wear a spare one from the office, and it said \"Brenda\" on it. \n\nAll day, customers kept calling me Brenda. \"Can I get some more coffee, Brenda?\" \"Check please, Brenda.\" It was so weird. The only Brenda I actually know is my mom's cousin, and she lives way out in Ohio. She collects those little porcelain dolls, you know the ones with the big glassy eyes and the painted-on eyelashes? She has a whole glass cabinet full of them in her living room. Whenever we'd visit when I was little, I was terrified of them. I always felt like they were watching me while I ate my macaroni and cheese. My mom made the absolute best macaroni and cheese, baked in the oven with the crispy breadcrumbs on top. She'd use three different kinds of cheese—sharp cheddar, gruyere, and I think parmesan. We'd always have it for Thanksgiving instead of stuffing because my younger brother was allergic to celery, and you really can't make good traditional stuffing without celery. He used to break out in hives if he even touched a piece of it. We had to rush him to the emergency room once when he was seven because he ate some potato salad at a church picnic that had hidden celery in it... \n\nBut anyway, no, I always feel like myself. I'm definitely not Brenda, but I'm me. Everything feels fine with who I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not really living your life, but rather just going through the motions?\n\nPatient: Going through the motions? I mean, I guess work can feel a little repetitive sometimes, but I wouldn't say I'm not living my life. I'm definitely living it. Although, rolling silverware at the end of a shift is the definition of going through the motions.
